B.1.1
The RR sector and the topological term of the type IIA action

Below, we plug the field content Eq. (
B.4
) into the different terms of the R-R sector. For the 2-form we find

-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\hat{F}_{2}\wedge\star\hat{F}_{2}=-\frac{\mathcal{V}}{2}dA^{0}\wedge\star_{4}dA^{0}\ ,

(B.6)

where
\mathcal{V}
is the volume of
\mathcal{X}
and
G_{AB}
is given by Eq. (
2.20
). The 4-form dimensional reduction reads

\displaystyle\hskip-30.00005pt-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\hat{F}_{4}\wedge\star\hat{F}_{4}=-\frac{1}{2}\left[\mathcal{V}\left(dC_{3}-B_{2}\wedge dA^{0}\right)\wedge\star_{4}\left(dC_{3}-B_{2}\wedge dA^{0}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+4\mathcal{V}G_{AB}\left(dA^{A}-b^{A}dA^{0}\right)\wedge\star_{4}\left(dA^{B}-b^{B}dA^{0}\right)

\displaystyle-(\text{Im}\ \mathcal{M})^{-1\ \hat{K}\hat{L}}\left(d\tilde{\xi}_{\hat{K}}+\mathcal{M}_{\hat{K}\hat{M}}d\xi^{\hat{M}}\right)\wedge\star_{4}\left(d\tilde{\xi}_{\hat{L}}+\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{\hat{L}\hat{N}}d\xi^{\hat{N}}\right)\ ,

(B.7)

where we have used
G^{AB}:=4\mathcal{V}\int\tilde{\omega}^{A}\wedge\star\tilde{\omega}^{B}
is the inverse metric of

2.35
) and given below for convenience

\displaystyle\int\alpha_{\hat{K}}\wedge\star\alpha_{\hat{L}}=-\left[\text{Im}\ \mathcal{M}+(\text{Re}\ \mathcal{M})(\text{Im}\ \mathcal{M})^{-1}(\text{Re}\ \mathcal{M})\right]_{\hat{K}\hat{L}}\ ,

(B.8)

\displaystyle\int\beta^{\hat{K}}\wedge\star\beta^{\hat{L}}=-\left(\text{Im}\ \mathcal{M}\right)^{-1\ \hat{K}\hat{L}}\ ,

(B.9)

\displaystyle\int\alpha_{\hat{K}}\wedge\star\beta^{\hat{L}}=-\left[(\text{Re}\ \mathcal{M})(\text{Im}\ \mathcal{M})^{-1}\right]_{\hat{K}}^{\hat{L}}\ .

(B.10)

Finally the topological term is

\displaystyle\hskip-50.00008pt\int_{\mathcal{X}}\hat{B}_{2}\wedge d\hat{C}_{3}\wedge d\hat{C}_{3}-(\hat{B}_{2})^{2}\wedge d\hat{C}_{3}\wedge d\hat{C}_{1}+{1\over 3}(\hat{B}_{2})^{3}\wedge d\hat{C}_{1}\wedge d\hat{C}_{1}=

(B.11)

\displaystyle B_{2}\wedge d\left(\tilde{\xi}_{\hat{K}}d\xi^{\hat{K}}-\xi^{\hat{L}}d\tilde{\xi}_{\hat{L}}\right)+\mathcal{K}_{ABC}b^{A}\left[dA^{B}\wedge dA^{C}-b^{B}dA^{0}\wedge\left(dA^{C}-{1\over 3}b^{C}dA^{0}\right)\right]\ .

The action can now be written in terms of the gauge-coupling matrices defined in Eq. (
2.24
)

\displaystyle\text{Re}\mathcal{N}=\begin{pmatrix}-\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{K}_{ABC}b^{A}b^{B}b^{C}&\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}_{ABC}b^{A}b^{B}\\
\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}_{ABC}b^{A}b^{B}&-\mathcal{K}_{ABC}b^{C}\end{pmatrix}\ ,

(B.12)

\displaystyle\text{Im}\mathcal{N}=-\mathcal{V}\begin{pmatrix}1+4G_{AB}b^{A}b^{B}&-4G_{AB}b^{B}\\
-4G_{AB}b^{B}&4G_{AB}\end{pmatrix}\ ,

(B.13)

\displaystyle(\text{Im}\mathcal{N})^{-1}=-\frac{1}{\mathcal{V}}\begin{pmatrix}1&b^{A}\\
b^{A}&\frac{1}{4}G^{AB}+b^{A}b^{B}\end{pmatrix}\ .

(B.14)

Before doing so, we will first dualise the
B_{2}
and
C_{3}
fields into a scalar
a(x)
and a constant flux
e_{0}
, respectively. This will allow us to put the
\mathcal{N}=2
action in its gauged form
[
370
]
, turning a double tensor multiplet containing
B_{2}
into a hypermultiplet in terms of
a
.

B.1.2
Dualising the
B_{2}
and
C_{3}
fields

Let us write the Lagrangians we are trying to dualise explicitly and work from there. Starting from the
C_{3}
gauge field, we collect all pieces containing
C_{3}
in

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{C_{3}}=-\frac{\mathcal{V}}{2}\left(dC_{3}-B_{2}\wedge dA^{0}\right)\wedge\star_{4}\left(dC_{3}-B_{2}\wedge dA^{0}\right)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{\mathcal{V}}{2}\left(dC_{3}-J_{4}\right)\wedge\star_{4}\left(dC_{3}-J_{4}\right)-e_{0}\left(dC_{3}-0\right)\ ,

(B.15)

where we have introduced
J_{4}
for notational convenience. The Lagrange multiplier
e_{0}
enforces the (trivial) Bianchi identity
1
1

1

Note that if one tries to simply find the equation of motion for
\star C_{3}
and substitute back, because
dC_{3}
is a top-form then the equations of motion would contain a derivative of a top-form which must vanish identically and this method does not work. One must introduce the Lagrange multiplier in the case of a top-form field strength.
. The equation of motion for
dC_{3}
becomes

\frac{\mathcal{V}}{2}\star_{4}\left(dC_{3}-J_{4}\right)=-e_{0}\quad\Rightarrow\quad dC_{3}=J_{4}+\frac{e_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}\star_{4}{\bf 1}

(B.16)

and substituting back

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{e_{0}}=-\frac{e_{0}^{2}}{2\mathcal{V}}\star_{4}\mathbf{1}-e_{0}J_{4}=-\frac{e_{0}^{2}}{2\mathcal{V}}\star_{4}\mathbf{1}-e_{0}\left(B_{2}\wedge dA^{0}\right)\ .

(B.17)

Next, we can substitute the expression back into the Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}_{C_{3}}
to obtain the dual description. We also choose to group the field strength fields present in the theory as

F^{\hat{A}}:=\{dA^{0},dA^{A}\}\ ,

(B.18)

for later convenience. Finally, we would like to dualise the
dB_{2}
field strength into the scalar
a(x)
. This follows from our results in Sec.
A.2
, which we recall below for convenience. In this case, the Lagrangian for the
H_{3}
field strength reads

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{H_{3}}=-\frac{1}{4}dB_{2}\wedge\star dB_{2}+\frac{1}{2}dB_{2}\wedge\left(\tilde{\xi}_{\hat{K}}d\xi^{\hat{K}}-\xi^{\hat{L}}d\tilde{\xi}_{\hat{L}}\right)\ ,

(B.19)

the equation of motion for
B_{2}
implies

d\left[{1\over 2}\star H_{3}-\left(\tilde{\xi}_{\hat{K}}d\xi^{\hat{K}}-\xi^{\hat{L}}d\tilde{\xi}_{\hat{L}}\right)\right]=0\quad\Rightarrow\quad da=\frac{1}{2}\star dB_{2}-\left(\tilde{\xi}_{\hat{K}}d\xi^{\hat{K}}-\xi^{\hat{L}}d\tilde{\xi}_{\hat{L}}\right)\ ,

(B.20)

which, together with Eq. (
B.6
), Eq. (
B.7
), Eq. (
B.11
), Eq. (
B.16
), can be substituted in Eq. (
B.1
) to find the four-dimensional type IIA effective action
2
2

2

Where a similar Weyl rescaling has been done to put the action in Einstein frame, similar to the one done on the IIB side.

[
370
]

\displaystyle\hskip-25.00003pt\mathcal{S}_{IIA}^{(4)}=\int-\frac{1}{2}R\star\mathbf{1}+\frac{1}{2}\text{Im}\ \mathcal{N}_{\hat{A}\hat{B}}F^{\hat{A}}\wedge\star F^{\hat{B}}+\frac{1}{2}\text{Re}\ \mathcal{N}_{\hat{A}\hat{B}}F^{\hat{A}}\wedge F^{\hat{B}}

\displaystyle-G_{AB}dT^{A}\wedge\star d\bar{T}^{B}-h_{uv}d\tilde{q}^{u}\wedge\star d\tilde{q}^{v}-V\star{\bf 1}\ ,

(B.21)

with

V={e^{4\phi}\over 2\mathcal{V}}e_{0}^{2}\ ,

(B.22)

and

\displaystyle h_{uv}d\tilde{q}^{u}\wedge\star d\tilde{q}^{v}:=d\phi\wedge\star d\phi+G_{K\bar{L}}dz^{K}\wedge\star d\bar{z}^{L}+

\displaystyle\frac{e^{4\phi}}{4}\left(Da+\tilde{\xi}_{\hat{K}}d\xi^{\hat{K}}-\xi^{\hat{L}}d\tilde{\xi}_{\hat{L}}\right)\wedge\star\left(Da+\tilde{\xi}_{\hat{K}}d\xi^{\hat{K}}-\xi^{\hat{L}}d\tilde{\xi}_{\hat{L}}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{e^{2\phi}}{2}(\text{Im}\ \mathcal{M})^{-1\ \hat{K}\hat{L}}\left(d\tilde{\xi}_{\hat{K}}+\mathcal{M}_{\hat{K}\hat{M}}d\xi^{\hat{M}}\right)\wedge\star\left(d\tilde{\xi}_{\hat{L}}+\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{\hat{L}\hat{N}}d\xi^{\hat{N}}\right)\ ,

(B.23)

where
Da:=da+2e_{0}A^{0}
. We have dropped the subindex on the four-dimensional Hodge star, since there are no more ambiguities. Notably, the scalar potential of the type IIA
\mathcal{N}=2
is not flat due to the presence of the Freund-Rubin
flux

e_{0}
, which is the gauge charge for the axion
a
dual to the external piece of the Kalb-Ramond two-form. The spectrum of the theory is given in Tab.
B.2
.

B.2
Orientifolding and
\mathcal{N}=1
type IIA supergravity

Similar to the type IIB case, we must reduce the supergravity spectrum by halving the number of supersymmetry generators through an orientifold projection.

We write down the orientifold projection operator as
[
31
]

\mathcal{O}=\Omega_{p}\left(-1\right)^{\text{F${}_{\text{L}}$}}\sigma\ .

(B.24)

If one wishes to preserve
\mathcal{N}=1
supersymmetry, then the involution must act on the Kähler form and the holomorphic 3-form as

\sigma^{*}J=-J\ ,\qquad\sigma^{*}\Omega=e^{2i\theta}\bar{\Omega}\ ,

(B.25)

for a constant phase
e^{2i\theta}
and where
\sigma^{*}
is the pullback of
\sigma
, an anti-holomorphic involution acting on
\mathcal{X}
. This in contrast with the type IIB orientifold conditions where the involution is holomorphic. In type IIA supergravity, the above conditions are consistent with the introduction of
O6
-planes in the theory.

We have seen that the effect of the involution on the cohomology of
\mathcal{X}
is to split them in even and odd cohomologies

H^{p,\,q}=H^{p,\,q}_{+}\oplus H^{p,\,q}_{-}\ ,

(B.26)

such that an element
\omega_{\pm}\in H^{p,\,q}_{\pm}
transforms like
\sigma^{*}\omega_{\pm}=\pm\omega_{\pm}
. From the type IIA orientifold conditions Eq. (
B.25
), we can further see that

•

\sigma^{*}J=-J
implies
h^{3,\,3}_{-}=1
, since
\mathcal{V}\propto\int J\wedge J\wedge J
and
h^{0,\,0}_{+}=1
, by Hodge duality. Alternatively, this implies that
h^{3,\,3}_{+}=h^{0,\,0}_{-}=0
.

•

Due to the anti-holomorphicity of
\sigma
and Hodge duality, we find
h^{1,\,1}_{\pm}=h^{2,\,2}_{\mp}
and
h^{3}_{+}=h^{3}_{-}=h^{2,\,1}+1
.

In Tab.
B.3
we summarise the notation for the truncated harmonic bases after orientifolding, which largely follows the notation of the type IIB analogues,

:

\mathcal{X}
.

where
\hat{k}=0,...,\tilde{h}
and
\lambda=\tilde{h},...,h^{2,\,1}
. The non-trivial intersection numbers are given by

\int_{\mathcal{X}}\omega_{a}\wedge\tilde{\omega}^{b}=\delta_{a}^{b}\ ,\qquad\int_{\mathcal{X}}\omega_{\alpha}\wedge\tilde{\omega}^{\beta}=\delta_{\alpha}^{\beta}\ ,\qquad\int_{\mathcal{X}}\alpha_{\hat{k}}\wedge\beta^{\hat{l}}=\delta_{\hat{k}}^{\hat{l}}\ ,\qquad\int_{\mathcal{X}}\alpha_{\kappa}\wedge\beta^{\lambda}=\delta_{\kappa}^{\lambda}\ .

(B.27)

The action of the parity operator and the fermion number operator on the 10-dimensional fields is summarised in Tab.
B.4

[
35
]
. It is clear from it that the invariant orientifolded states must obey

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}\hat{\phi}=+\hat{\phi}\ ,\qquad\sigma^{*}\hat{g}=+\hat{g}\ ,\qquad\sigma^{*}\hat{B_{2}}=-\hat{B_{2}}\ ,

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}\hat{C_{1}}=-\hat{C_{1}}\ ,\qquad\sigma^{*}\hat{C_{3}}=+\hat{C_{3}}\ .

(B.28)

:

One can truncate the
\mathcal{N}=2
spectrum as follows. For the Kähler structure we obtain

J=v^{a}\omega_{a}\ ,\quad B_{2}=b^{a}\omega_{a}\ ,\quad J_{c}:=J-iB_{2}=T^{a}\omega_{a}:=(v-ib)^{a}\omega_{a}\ .

(B.29)

Note, in particular, that the orientifold has projected out the external
B_{2}(x)
piece, which before we had dualised into a gauge scalar charged under
e_{0}
. The complex structure deformations are given by applying the orientifold constraint to obtain the conditions

\text{Im}\ (C\mathcal{Z}^{\hat{k}})=\text{Re}\ (C\mathcal{F}_{\hat{k}})=0\ ,\quad\text{Re}\ (C\mathcal{Z}^{\lambda})=\text{Im}\ \left(C\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}\right)=0\qquad C:=e^{-\phi-i\theta}e^{K^{cs}/2}

(B.30)

so that the expansion of the holomorphic three-form with the regulator function
C
is

C\Omega=\text{Re}\ (C\mathcal{Z}^{\hat{k}})\alpha_{\hat{k}}+i\text{Im}\ (C\mathcal{Z}^{\lambda})\alpha_{\lambda}-\text{Re}\ (C\mathcal{F}_{\lambda})\beta^{\lambda}-i\text{Im}\ (C\mathcal{F}_{{\hat{k}}})\beta^{{\hat{k}}}\ .

(B.31)

One can still choose the Kähler gauge so that
\mathcal{Z}^{0}=1
, however it is most convenient to keep the gauge freedom for now while keeping in mind that there are only
h^{2,1}
degrees of freedom in the complex structure sector. Finally, let us look at the one and three-form gauge field in the RR sector.

The involution constraints impose that

\sigma^{*}\hat{A}_{1}=-\hat{A}_{1}\ ,\quad\rightarrow\quad\hat{A}_{1}\in H^{1,0}(Y)\ .

(B.32)

If
\mathcal{X}
is a Calabi-Yau threefold, there are no non-trivial one-cycles and
\hat{A}_{1}
should at most be an external field. However, the orientifold acts trivially on any external fields, thus the
\mathcal{N}=2
graviphoton
A_{0}
must be projected out.

Finally,

\sigma^{*}\hat{C}_{3}=\hat{C}_{3}\ ,\quad\rightarrow\quad\hat{C}_{3}\in H^{3}_{+}(Y)\ .

(B.33)

In this case,
\hat{C}_{3}
admits an external form
C_{3}
, that will dualise once again into the Freud-Rubin flux
e_{0}
, such that the gauge field can be expanded into

\hat{C}_{3}=C_{3}(x)+A^{\alpha}\wedge\omega_{\alpha}+\xi^{\hat{k}}\alpha_{\hat{k}}-\tilde{\xi}_{\kappa}\beta^{\kappa}:=C_{3}(x)+A^{\alpha}\wedge\omega_{\alpha}+C^{i}_{3}(x)

(B.34)

defining a complexified three-form

\Omega_{c}:=C^{i}_{3}+2i\text{Re}\ \left(C\Omega\right)\ ,\qquad C:=e^{-\phi-i\theta}e^{K^{cs}/2}\ .

(B.35)

Including the dilaton through the compensator function
C
is equivalent to trading the irrelevant scale factor of the three-form
\Omega
by the dilaton, so that all
h^{2,\,1}+1
fields in the definition of
\Omega_{c}
are physical. This will make it so introducing coordinates to the quaternionic manifold later on can be done directly through
\Omega_{c}
.

Much like in type IIB analogue, locally the moduli space can still be written in a product form of two Kähler manifolds

\tilde{\mathcal{M}}^{ks}\times\tilde{\mathcal{M}}^{Q}\ ,

(B.36)

with
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}^{ks}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}^{Q}
subspaces of the
\mathcal{N}=2
Kähler and quaternionic manifolds, respectively. In contrast with the type IIB, the complex structure manifold is now part of the quaternionic manifold,
\mathcal{M}^{cs}\in\mathcal{M}^{Q}
. This would be expected from the usual duality arguments, where the Kähler sector of type IIB maps to a dual complex structure sector in IIA and vice versa (for explicit details of the mirror symmetry see, for example,
[
371
,
31
]
, and references therein). Now we would like to study the effects of the orientifold projection onto the Kähler and quaternionic sector following the conditions laid out before to obtain the
\mathcal{N}=1
effective action.

B.2.1
The Kähler structure of
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}^{ks}

The number of Kähler moduli
h^{1,1}
is reduced in half to
h^{1,1}_{-}
,
i.e.

t^{A}\,\rightarrow\,t^{a}\ ,\qquad\omega_{A}\,\rightarrow\,\omega_{a}\ ,\qquad a=1,...,h^{1,\,1}_{-}.

(B.37)

Note that these projections imply some vanishing of the triple intersection numbers. Indeed, recall that
d\mathcal{V}\in H^{6}_{-}
which means that

\mathcal{K}_{ABC}v^{A}v^{B}v^{C}:=\int_{\mathcal{X}}v^{A}v^{B}v^{C}\omega_{A}\wedge\omega_{B}\wedge\omega_{C}\propto\int_{\mathcal{X}}d\mathcal{V}\ ,

(B.38)

so the integrand must be odd under the action of
\sigma
and thus only intersection numbers with one or three odd-legs are non-trivial after the projection. Similarly, one can apply the same reasoning to the
contractions
of the intersection numbers,
i.e.

\mathcal{K}_{AB}
and
\mathcal{K}_{A}
, together with the fact that
J\in H^{1,1}_{-}
to find

\mathcal{K}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=\mathcal{K}_{\alpha ab}=\mathcal{K}_{\alpha a}=\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}=0\ .

(B.39)

Then, the
\mathcal{N}=1
Kähler metric can be obtained directly by using the conditions above on the
\mathcal{N}=2
Kähler metric in Eq. (
2.20
). The orientifolded metric takes the block-diagonal form

\displaystyle G_{ab}=-\frac{1}{4\mathcal{V}}\left(\mathcal{K}_{ab}-\frac{\mathcal{K}_{a}\mathcal{K}_{b}}{4\mathcal{V}}\right)\ ,\qquad G_{\alpha\beta}=-\frac{\mathcal{K}_{\alpha\beta}}{4\mathcal{V}}\ ,

(B.40)

\displaystyle G_{\alpha a}=0\ .

(B.41)

The same constraints can be applied to the gauge-kinetic coupling matrix
\mathcal{N}_{\hat{A}\hat{B}}
to get

\displaystyle\text{Re}\ \mathcal{N}_{{\alpha}{\beta}}=-\mathcal{K}_{\alpha\beta a}b^{a}\ ,\qquad\text{Im}\ \mathcal{N}_{{\alpha}{\beta}}=\mathcal{K}_{\alpha\beta}\ ,

(B.42)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{0\alpha}=\mathcal{N}_{a\alpha}=0\ ,

(B.43)

plus the components
\mathcal{N}_{\hat{a}\hat{b}}
found by substituting
\left(\hat{A},\hat{B},\hat{C}\right)\,\rightarrow\,\left(\hat{a},\hat{b},\hat{c}\right)
, with
\hat{a}=0,a
, in the formulae of Eq. (
2.25
), Eq. (
2.26
) and Eq. (
2.27
). Finally, the Kähler potential is simply given by

K^{ks}=-\ln\left[\frac{1}{6}\mathcal{K}_{abc}\left(T+\bar{T}\right)^{a}\left(T+\bar{T}\right)^{b}\left(T+\bar{T}\right)^{c}\right]=-\ln\left(\bar{T}^{\hat{a}}\partial_{T^{\hat{a}}}F-{T}^{\hat{a}}\partial_{\bar{T}^{\hat{a}}}\bar{F}\right)\ ,

(B.44)

with
\hat{a}=0,1,...,h^{1,\,1}_{-}
the adapted coordinates of
\mathcal{M}^{ks}
and the prepotential function that generates the Kähler potential is
F=\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{K}_{abc}{T^{a}T^{b}T^{c}\over T^{0}}
. The
\mathcal{N}=1
Kähler sector for the orientifolded effective action reads

\mathcal{S}_{IIA}^{(4)}=\int-G_{ab}\,dT^{a}\wedge\star d\bar{T}^{b}+\frac{1}{2}\text{Im}\ \mathcal{N}_{\alpha\beta}F^{\alpha}\wedge\star F^{\beta}+\frac{1}{2}\text{Re}\ \mathcal{N}_{\alpha\beta}F^{\alpha}\wedge F^{\beta}\ ,

(B.45)

with
F^{\alpha}:=dA^{\alpha}
.

B.2.2
The quaternionic structure of
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}^{Q}

The quaternionic sector is more involved. The complex structure deformations complying with the orientifolds constraints are given by considering infinitesimal variations of
\Omega
and making use of the Kodaira identity Eq. (
2.29
) as

\Omega\left(z+\delta z\right)=\Omega(z)+\delta z^{K}\left(\partial_{z^{K}}\Omega\right)(z)=\Omega(z)-\delta z^{K}\left(\partial_{z^{K}}K^{cs}\Omega-\chi^{K}\right)(z)\ .

(B.46)

We require that both
\Omega(z)
and
\Omega(z+\delta z)
comply with the orientifold constraints Eq. (
B.25
). Also notice that the complex conjugate to the holomorphic three-form is given by

\hskip-14.22636pt\bar{\Omega}(\bar{z}+\delta\bar{z})=\bar{\Omega}(\bar{z})-\delta\bar{z}^{K}\left(\partial_{\bar{z}^{K}}\bar{K}^{cs}\bar{\Omega}-\bar{\chi}^{K}\right)(\bar{z})=\bar{\Omega}(\bar{z})-\delta\bar{z}^{K}\left(\partial_{\bar{z}^{K}}K^{cs}\bar{\Omega}-\bar{\chi}^{K}\right)(\bar{z})\ ,

(B.47)

since
K^{cs}:=-i\ln\left(i\int\Omega\wedge\bar{\Omega}\right)=\bar{K}^{cs}
by the odd properties of the external product on odd-forms. This implies that locally

\delta z^{K}\partial_{z^{K}}K^{cs}=\delta\bar{z}^{K}\partial_{\bar{z}^{K}}K^{cs}\ ,\qquad\delta z^{K}\sigma^{*}\chi^{K}=e^{2i\theta}\delta\bar{z}^{K}\bar{\chi}^{K}\ ,

(B.48)

where we have used that the involution acts trivially on external pieces so that

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}\left[\delta z^{K}\left(\partial_{z^{K}}K^{cs}\right)\Omega\right]=\delta z^{K}\left(\partial_{z^{K}}K^{cs}\right)\left(\sigma^{*}\Omega\right)\ ,

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}\left(\delta z^{K}\chi^{K}\right)=\delta z^{K}\left(\sigma^{*}\chi^{K}\right)\ ,

From the first equality of Eq. (
B.48
) and recalling that
K^{cs}
is a Kähler potential,
i.e.

\partial_{z^{K}}K^{cs}\neq 0
, we find that for each
\delta z^{K}
either its real or its imaginary part has to be zero. The number of degrees of freedom has been halved and one may choose any basis
z^{K}=\left(z^{k},z^{\lambda}\right)
with
\text{Im}\ z^{k}=\text{Re}\ z^{\lambda}=0
for any splitting of
h^{2,1}+1
in
k
-components and
\lambda
-components. We will keep this splitting generic for now.

These coordinates
z^{K}=\left(z^{k},z^{\lambda}\right)
can now be mapped to adapted coordinates of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\text{cs}}
by the embedding
[
31
]

\displaystyle\rho:\qquad\qquad\mathcal{M}^{cs}_{\mathbb{R}}\qquad

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow

\displaystyle\qquad\mathcal{M}^{cs}

\displaystyle q^{K}=(z^{\hat{k}},z^{\lambda})\qquad

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\qquad z^{K}=\left(z^{\hat{k}},iz^{\lambda}\right)

and its metric is given by

\rho^{*}\left(G_{K\bar{L}}dz^{K}\wedge\star d\bar{z}^{L}\right)=G_{KL}(q)dq^{K}\wedge\star dq^{L}\ ,\qquad\rho^{*}\left(iG_{K\bar{L}}dz^{K}\wedge d\bar{z}^{L}\right)=0\ .

(B.49)

with the period matrices given by restricting

\mathcal{M}_{\hat{K}\hat{L}}:=\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{\hat{K}\hat{L}}+2i\frac{(\text{Im}\ \mathcal{F})_{\hat{K}\hat{M}}\;\mathcal{Z}^{\hat{M}}(\text{Im}\ \mathcal{F})_{\hat{L}\hat{N}}\;\mathcal{Z}^{\hat{N}}}{\mathcal{Z}^{\hat{M}}(\text{Im}\ \mathcal{F})_{\hat{M}\hat{N}}\mathcal{Z}^{\hat{N}}}\ ,

(B.50)

to
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}^{cs}_{\mathbb{R}}
and applying the constraints

\text{Im}\ (C\mathcal{Z}^{\hat{k}})=\text{Re}\ \left(C\mathcal{F}_{\hat{k}}\right)=0\ ,\quad\text{Re}\ (C\mathcal{Z}^{\lambda})=\text{Im}\ \left(C\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}\right)=0\ ,

(B.51)

we find

\text{Re}\ \mathcal{M}_{\sigma\lambda}(q)=\text{Re}\ \mathcal{M}_{\hat{k}\hat{l}}(q)=\text{Im}\ \mathcal{M}_{\hat{k}\sigma}(q)=0\ ,

(B.52)

Finally, the Kähler potential for the quaternionic manifold is
[
31
,
372
]

\displaystyle K^{Q}=-2\ln\left[2\int_{Y}\text{Re}\ \left(C\Omega\right)\wedge\star\text{Re}\ \left(C\Omega\right)\right]

\displaystyle=-2\ln 2\left[\text{Re}\ (CF_{\lambda})\text{Im}\ (CZ^{\lambda})-\text{Im}\ (CF_{\hat{k}})\text{Re}\ (CZ^{\hat{k}})\right]=-\ln e^{-4\phi}\ .

(B.53)

For future reference, when writing the supergravity action in term of chiral supermultiplets, it will be convenient to define the coordinates of the quaternionic manifold
\mathcal{M}^{Q}
in terms of half of the periods of
\Omega_{c}

[
31
]

\displaystyle N^{\hat{k}}:=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\Omega_{c}\wedge\beta^{\hat{k}}=\xi^{\hat{k}}+2i\text{Re}\ (CZ^{\hat{k}})\ ,

(B.54)

\displaystyle U_{\lambda}:=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\Omega_{c}\wedge\alpha_{\lambda}=\tilde{\xi}_{\lambda}+2i\text{Re}\ (C\mathcal{F}_{\lambda})\ .

(B.55)

B.3
The type IIA
\mathcal{N}=1
effective action

The orientifolded effective action is given by
[
31
]

\displaystyle\hskip-25.00003pt\mathcal{S}_{IIA,\,\mathcal{N}=1}^{(4)}=\int-\frac{1}{2}R\star\mathbf{1}+\frac{1}{2}\text{Im}\ \mathcal{N}_{\alpha\beta}F^{\alpha}\wedge\star F^{\beta}+\frac{1}{2}\text{Re}\ \mathcal{N}_{\alpha\beta}F^{\alpha}\wedge F^{\beta}

\displaystyle-G_{ab}\,dT^{a}\wedge\star d\bar{T}^{b}-h_{uv}dq^{u}\wedge\star dq^{v}-V\star\mathbf{1}\ ,

(B.56)

with the same Freund-Rubin potential

V={e^{4\phi}\over 2\mathcal{V}}e_{0}^{2}\ ,

(B.57)

and where

\displaystyle h_{uv}d\tilde{q}^{u}\wedge\star d\tilde{q}^{v}=d\phi\wedge\star d\phi+G_{KL}(q)dz^{K}\wedge\star dz^{L}-\frac{e^{2\phi}}{2}\left(\text{Im}\ \mathcal{M}\right)_{\hat{k}\hat{l}}d\xi^{\hat{k}}\wedge\star d\xi^{\hat{l}}

\displaystyle-\frac{e^{2\phi}}{2}\left(\text{Im}\ \mathcal{M}\right)^{-1\,\sigma\lambda}\left(d\tilde{\xi}_{\sigma}-\text{Re}\ \mathcal{M}_{\hat{k}\sigma}d\xi^{\hat{k}}\right)\wedge\star\left(d\tilde{\xi}_{\lambda}-\text{Re}\ \mathcal{M}_{\hat{k}\lambda}d\xi^{\hat{k}}\right)\ ,

(B.58)

is now the metric adapted to the orientifolded quaternionic manifold, of which
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{R}}^{cs}\in\mathcal{M}^{Q}
.

Analogously to the type IIB case, we are now presented with the problem of defining appropriate coordinates for the quaternionic manifold. This can be done in a couple different ways. In
[
31
]
, the author defines coordinates based on the holomorphic periods of the truncated theory. Another approach is to think of the appropriate coordinates as those dual
3
3

3

There are some subtleties due to the broken symplectic invariance in an orientifolded type IIA theory and the choice of orientifolds planes in the type IIB dual, see
[
31
]
.
to the Kähler sector of the mirror of
\mathcal{X}
, like in
[
372
]
. Below we present the results

•

Axio-dilaton:
N^{0}:=S=s+i\sigma=\xi^{0}+2i\text{Re}\ (CZ^{0})=\int\Omega_{c}\wedge\beta^{0}
.

•

Kähler moduli:
T^{a}=v^{a}+ib^{a}
, with
a=1,...,h^{1,\,1}_{-}

•

Even complex structure moduli:
U_{\lambda}:=u_{\lambda}+i\nu_{\lambda}=\tilde{\xi}_{\lambda}+2i\text{Re}\ (C\mathcal{F}_{\lambda})=\int\Omega_{c}\wedge\alpha_{\lambda}
.

•

Odd complex structure moduli:
N^{k}:=n^{k}+i\eta^{k}:=\xi^{k}+2i\text{Re}\ (CZ^{k})=\int\Omega_{c}\wedge\beta^{k}
.

The odd complex structure sector is the dual to the odd Kähler sector in type IIB. To make contact with the same type of theories, we finally make use of the symplectic freedom to choose
N^{k}=0
. This implies that
H^{3}(\mathcal{X})=H^{2,\,1}_{+}\oplus H^{3,\,0}_{+}
, and
\lambda=1,...,h^{2,1}_{+}
. We also remark that the Kähler moduli have been defined in terms of the two-cycle volume moduli
v^{a}
in contrast with type IIB case, which was defined in terms of the four-cycle volume moduli
\tau_{\alpha}
Eq. (
2.60
).

With this splitting, the Kähler potential of the truncated theory is given by

\displaystyle K=K^{ks}+K^{Q}\ ,

(B.59)

\displaystyle K^{ks}=-\ln\left[\frac{1}{6}\mathcal{K}_{abc}\left(T+\bar{T}\right)^{a}\left(T+\bar{T}\right)^{b}\left(T+\bar{T}\right)^{c}\right]\ ,

(B.60)

\displaystyle K^{Q}=-2\ln\left[2\int_{\mathcal{X}}\text{Re}\ (C\Omega)\wedge\star\text{Re}\ (C\Omega)\right]\ ,

(B.61)

and the moduli space is, once again, given locally by the direct product
4
4

4

Notice that the moduli space will always be given by a direct product of the Kähler sector and the quaternionic sector, no matter the choice of symplectic gauge.

\mathcal{M}^{ks}_{h^{1,\,1}_{-}}\times\mathcal{M}^{Q}_{h^{2,1}_{+}+1}\ .

(B.62)

Finally, we would like to put this in the
\mathcal{N}=1
gauged supergravity form of
[
34
]

\hskip-30.00005pt\mathcal{S}^{(4)}_{IIA,\,\mathcal{N}=1}=-\int{1\over 2}R\star{\mathbf{1}}+K_{I\bar{J}}DM^{I}\wedge\star D\bar{M}^{\bar{J}}+{1\over 4}\text{Re}\ \mathcal{N}_{\alpha\beta}F^{\alpha}\wedge F^{\beta}+{1\over 4}\text{Im}\ \mathcal{N}_{\alpha\beta}F^{\alpha}\wedge\star F^{\beta}+V\star\mathbf{1}\ ,

(B.63)

where the scalar potential is given by

V=e^{K}\left(K^{I\bar{J}}D_{I}WD_{\bar{J}}\bar{W}-3\left|W\right|^{2}\right)\ ,

(B.64)

and we have followed the common notation for the chiral fields and Kähler derivatives described below Eq. (
2.65
). In this case, the scalar potential is non-zero but only contains a cosmological constant-like contribution from the Freund-Rubin flux
e_{0}
Eq. (
B.22
). Like in the type IIB case, we would like to introduce background fluxes that will allow us to stabilise some moduli.

B.4
Type IIA flux compactifications

In this section we will provide the effective action in the case where NSNS and RR background fluxes are turned on. Furthermore, we will consider the case of massive type IIA supergravity by allowing for a non-zero Romans mass parameter
m

[
176
]
. The massive type IIA ten-dimensional action is

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{IIA-M}=-\int\left({1\over 2}\hat{R}\star\mathbf{1}+{1\over 4}d\hat{\phi}\wedge\star d\hat{\phi}+{1\over 4}e^{-\hat{\phi}}\hat{H}_{3}\wedge\star\hat{H}_{3}\right)

(B.65)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int\left(e^{3\hat{\phi}/2}\hat{F}_{2}\wedge\star\hat{F}_{2}+e^{\hat{\phi}/2}\hat{F}_{4}\wedge\star\hat{F}_{4}+e^{5\hat{\phi}/2}m^{2}\star\mathbf{1}+\mathcal{L}_{\text{top}}\right)\ ,

where the topological term now has Romans mass dependent pieces

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{top}}=\hat{B}_{2}\wedge d\hat{C}_{3}\wedge d\hat{C}_{3}-(\hat{B}_{2})^{2}\wedge d\hat{C}_{3}\wedge d\hat{C}_{1}+{1\over 3}(\hat{B}_{2})^{3}\wedge d\hat{C}_{1}\wedge d\hat{C}_{1}

(B.66)

\displaystyle m\left[{1\over 3}(\hat{B}_{2})^{3}\wedge d\hat{C}_{3}-{1\over 4}(\hat{B}_{2})^{4}\wedge d\hat{C}_{1}-{1\over 3}(\hat{B}_{2})^{5}\right]\ .

Similarly, the field strengths are now given by

\hat{H}_{3}=d\hat{B}_{2}\ ,\quad\hat{F}_{2}=d\hat{C}_{1}+m\hat{B}_{2}\ ,\quad\hat{F}_{4}=d\hat{C}_{3}-\hat{C}_{1}\wedge\hat{H}_{3}-{m\over 2}(\hat{B}_{2})^{2}\ ,

(B.67)

and the orientifold invariant fluxes will be given by

H_{3}^{(F)}=h_{0}\beta^{0}-h^{\lambda}\alpha_{\lambda}\ ,\quad F^{(F)}_{2}=q^{a}\omega_{a}\ ,\quad F_{4}^{F}=e_{a}\tilde{\omega}^{a}\ .

(B.68)

In
[
34
]
, it was shown that the scalar potential found from dimensional reduction can be obtained in terms of a superpotential

\displaystyle W=W_{K}+W_{Q}=\int_{\mathcal{X}}e^{-iJ_{c}}\wedge F^{(F)}_{RR}+\Omega_{c}\wedge H^{(F)}_{3}=

(B.69)

\displaystyle e_{0}-ie_{a}T^{a}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}_{abc}q^{a}T^{b}T^{c}-\frac{im}{6}\mathcal{K}_{abc}T^{a}T^{b}T^{c}+Sh_{0}+U_{\lambda}h^{\lambda}\ ,

and the Kähler potential Eq. (
B.59
). Here
F^{(F)}_{RR}
is the poly-form containing all RR background fluxes. The fluxed scalar potential is given by
[
172
]

\displaystyle V={R\over s^{4}}\left[\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}}{2\mathcal{V}}+\frac{G^{ab}\tilde{\rho}_{a}\tilde{\rho}_{b}}{8\mathcal{V}}+{2\mathcal{V}G_{ab}\rho^{a}\rho^{b}}+\frac{\mathcal{V}}{2}\rho_{m}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\mathcal{V}}\left(s^{2}h_{0}^{2}-\frac{1}{3}\left(u_{\lambda}h^{\lambda}\right)^{2}\right)\right]\ ,

(B.70)

where
R=16e^{2K^{cs}}\text{Re}\ (Z^{0})^{4}
, and we define the combination
\varphi:=e_{0}-\xi^{0}h_{0}+\tilde{\xi}_{\lambda}h^{\lambda}
with

\displaystyle\rho_{0}=\varphi+e_{a}b^{a}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}_{abc}q^{a}b^{b}b^{c}-{m\over 6}\mathcal{K}_{abc}b^{a}b^{b}b^{c},

(B.71)

\displaystyle-\rho_{a}=e_{a}+\mathcal{K}_{abc}q^{b}b^{c}-{m\over 2}\mathcal{K}_{abc}b^{b}b^{c},\quad\tilde{\rho}^{a}=q^{a}-mb^{a}\ ,\quad\rho_{m}=m\ ,

the dual scalars in the democratic prescription of type IIA
[
173
]
and use in Chap.
4
.

In contrast with the type IIA case, all moduli appear in the Kähler potential and superpotential. Thus, one might be hopeful that all fields could be stabilised at tree-level. However, on duality grounds we would expect some obstruction to this goal since the type IIB theory required access to non-tree-level corrections to stabilise the Kähler sector. We will study the stabilisation of the type IIA theory in Sec.
B.5
.

B.5
Moduli stabilisation in type IIA flux supergravity

In this section we follow the classic analysis of
[
177
]
. We will concentrate in studying the stabilisation of supersymmetric vacua configurations of type IIA. We will find that we are able to stabilise the dilaton, a linear combinations of axions in
\varphi
, the complex structure saxions
u_{\lambda}
and the Kähler sector, leaving
h^{2,\,1}
complex structure axions flat. To begin, we look for a solution to the F-term equations

D_{T}W=D_{S}W=D_{U}W=0\ .

(B.72)

Let us start with the quaternionic sector. The F-term for the axio-dilaton is

D_{S}W=h_{0}-{2iW\over e^{\sqrt{K^{Q}}}}\text{Im}\ \left(CF_{0}\right)=h_{0}-2iWe^{2\phi}\text{Im}\ \left(CF_{0}\right)=0\ ,

(B.73)

and for the complex moduli we find

D_{U^{\lambda}}=h^{\lambda}+{2iW\over e^{\sqrt{K^{Q}}}}\text{Im}\ (CZ^{\lambda})=h^{\lambda}+2iWe^{2\phi}\text{Im}\ (CZ^{\lambda})=0\ ,

(B.74)

where we have used the definitions of the chiral superfields in terms of the periods of
\Omega_{c}
so that

\displaystyle\partial_{S}=\partial_{2\text{Re}\ \left(CZ^{0}\right)}{\partial 2\text{Re}\ \left(CZ^{0}\right)\over\partial S}=-i\partial_{2\text{Re}\ \left(CZ^{0}\right)}\ ,

(B.75)

\displaystyle\partial_{U_{\lambda}}=\partial_{2\text{Re}\ \left(CF_{\lambda}\right)}{\partial 2\text{Re}\ \left(CF_{\lambda}\right)\over\partial U^{\lambda}}=-i\partial_{2\text{Re}\ \left(CF_{\lambda}\right)}\ .

(B.76)

Noting that the dilaton is real-valued, the imaginary parts of the quaternionic F-terms lead to the same condition

\text{Re}\ W=0\quad\rightarrow\quad h_{0}\sigma+\nu_{\lambda}h^{\lambda}+\text{Re}\ W_{K}=0\quad\text{or}\quad\varphi=-\text{Re}\ W_{K}\ .

(B.77)

These identical equations allows us to stabilise the dilaton and
\varphi
field at the minimum of the potential once we solve for the Kähler and complex structure fields. The degeneracy in these F-terms can be seen from the fact that we have
h^{2\,1}+1
complex equations and only
h^{2,\,1}+1
real fluxes in
\left(h_{0},h^{\lambda}\right)
, so there is not info degrees of freedom to stabilise the whole system.

Furthermore, we note that, after solving the previous F-terms

W=\text{Re}\ W+i\text{Im}\ W=i\text{Im}\ W\ ,

(B.78)

and thus
\text{Im}\ W=0
implies a vanishing superpotential, which is inconsistent with the presence of any fluxes. This implies that the only solution to the real part of Eq. (
B.73
) is given by

h_{0}+2e^{2\phi}\text{Im}\ W\ \text{Im}\ (CF_{0})=0\quad\rightarrow\quad e^{-\phi}=-2{\text{Im}\ W\over Q_{0}}\ ,

(B.79)

with
Q_{0}:=e^{-\sqrt{K^{cs}}}{h_{0}\over\text{Im}\ (F_{0})}
. Similarly, for the complex structure sector we find
\lambda
equations

h^{\lambda}-2e^{2\phi}\text{Im}\ W\ \text{Im}\ (CZ^{\lambda})=0\quad\rightarrow\quad{e^{-\phi}\over 2\text{Im}\ W}=e^{-\sqrt{K^{cs}}}{h^{\lambda}\over\text{Im}\ (Z^{\lambda})}:=-Q_{0}\ .

(B.80)

Then the chain of
h^{2,\,1}
equations

e^{-\sqrt{K^{cs}}}{h^{1}\over\text{Im}\ (Z^{1})}=e^{-\sqrt{K^{cs}}}{h^{2}\over\text{Im}\ (Z^{2})}=...=e^{-\sqrt{K^{cs}}}{h^{h^{2,\,1}}\over\text{Im}\ (Z^{h^{2,\,1}})}:=-Q_{0}\ ,

(B.81)

fixes the complex structure sector. On top of these, Eq. (
B.80
) will stabilise the ten-dimensional dilaton
\hat{\phi}
after the Kähler sector is fixed, given that the four-dimensional dilaton
\phi
contains a volume factor in its definition.

Before turning to the Kähler moduli, let us note that when the previous equations are satisfied the superpotential can be written in terms of its Kähler moduli only as

W(T^{a},S,U^{k})=-i\text{Im}\ W_{K}\left(T^{a}\right)\ ,

(B.82)

meaning the Kähler sector completely decouples from the complex structure sector and axio-dilaton. We can see this by multiplying Eq. (
B.73
) by
\text{Re}\ (CZ^{0})
and Eq. (
B.74
) by
\text{Re}\ (CF_{\lambda})
and adding them to obtain

\displaystyle-{1\over 2}\left(h_{0}\sigma+h^{\lambda}\nu_{\lambda}\right)-iWe^{2\phi}\left[2\left(\text{Re}\ (CF_{\lambda})\text{Im}\ (CZ^{\lambda})-\text{Im}\ (CF_{\hat{k}})\text{Re}\ (CZ^{\hat{k}})\right)\right]=

\displaystyle-{1\over 2}\text{Im}\ W^{Q}-iW=0\ .

(B.83)

The F-terms for the Kähler moduli are then

\displaystyle\hskip-110.00017ptD_{T^{a}}W=-ie_{a}+\mathcal{K}_{abc}q^{b}T^{c}-\frac{im}{2}\mathcal{K}_{abc}T^{b}T^{c}+i\frac{\text{Im}\ W_{K}}{2\mathcal{V}}\mathcal{K}_{abc}\left(T+\bar{T}\right)^{b}\left(T+\bar{T}\right)^{c}

\displaystyle=-ie_{a}+\mathcal{K}_{abc}q^{b}(v-ib)^{c}-\frac{im}{2}\mathcal{K}_{abc}(v^{b}v^{c}-b^{b}b^{c}-2ib^{b}v^{c})+i\frac{2\text{Im}\ W_{K}}{\mathcal{V}}\mathcal{K}_{abc}v^{b}v^{c}=0\ .

(B.84)

The real part leads to

\mathcal{K}_{abc}v^{c}\left(q^{b}-mb^{b}\right)=0\ .

(B.85)

Since
\mathcal{K}_{abc}v^{c}\propto G_{ab}
, the Kähler metric, for any given row there must at least be a non-vanishing component so that the metric is invertible. Thus, the object inside the parentheses must vanish yielding the stabilisation for the Kähler axions

b^{a}=\frac{q^{a}}{m}\ .

(B.86)

The imaginary part of the F-term for the Kähler sector gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{def}v^{d}v^{e}v^{f}\left(4me_{a}+2\mathcal{K}_{abc}q^{b}q^{c}+3m^{2}\mathcal{K}_{abc}v^{b}v^{c}\right)+

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{abc}v^{b}v^{c}\left(6me_{d}v^{d}+3\mathcal{K}_{def}q^{d}q^{e}v^{f}\right)=0\ ,

(B.87)

this gives
h^{1,1}
real-equations that are quadratic in
v^{a}
and allow us to stabilise them. We can also rewrite this equation in terms of the dual scalars from Eq. (
B.71
), after tracing over
v^{a}
, as

v^{a}\left(10me_{a}+5\mathcal{K}_{abc}q^{b}q^{c}+3m^{2}\mathcal{K}_{abc}v^{b}v^{c}\right)=v^{a}\left(10\rho_{a}+3m^{2}\mathcal{K}_{abc}v^{b}v^{c}\right)=0\ ,

(B.88)

Thus, the Kähler sector is fully stabilised, as opposed to the complex structure sector where only the saxions are fixed at the minimum. One can also understand this as the Kähler sector having
2\cdot h^{1,1}_{-}
fluxes,
(e_{a},q^{a})
, compared to the
h^{2,1}_{+}
fluxes of the complex structure sector,
h^{\lambda}
.

In contrast to the type IIB moduli stabilisation on Sec.
2.4
, we have been able to stabilise the Kähler sector, the axio-dilaton and the saxions of the complex structure sector. By duality arguments, however, we see that the complex structure saxions map to 4-volume cycles in the dual type IIB
[
371
,
31
]
, which had flat profiles at the same level of the perturbative expansion. In
[
170
]
, it was shown that turning on any of the
h^{\lambda}
fluxes required to stabilise the complex structure sector leads to a half-flat manifold and to non-geometric fluxes in the dual type IIB setting. This fluxes are not fully understood and present a problem regarding the trustability of solutions that employ them as means to stabilise moduli.

In Sec.
4.5
, we present a no-go theorem for the embedding of clockwork into perturbative type IIA theories. We use a form of the potential that formally contains this fluxes, but the argument is entirely focused on the Kähler sector and the impossibility of generating the clockwork mechanism through those. The argument only requires a decoupling of the Kähler and complex structure sector, which is true at tree-level in any case, to go through. Therefore, the trustability issues are not a factor for our discussion.
